ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e6798	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! More than 700 000 Austrians have signed the popular desire for a strong social state of Austria, giving a clear signal of the direction in which future policies are to develop. In this context, they have also expressed their dissatisfaction with the social disassembly carried out by the Blue-Black Government in Austria. As the initiators and promoters of popular desire are in the house, I would like to thank them very much for their initiative and welcome them to us. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Social peace and social stability have so far been part of the Austrian identity and have been inseparably linked to the Austrian path. What does social state and social stability mean? – This is an organisational principle of our society, which follows the following pattern: A society agrees on what we have to do together – on health, on education, on poverty prevention, on pension security and on other areas. The pattern of the organization of the social state works in such a way that everyone contributes to it according to his/her material possibilities: The person who has more makes a greater contribution, the person who has less, makes a smaller contribution. It is not only Austria, but also many other western European societies that have grown up with this principle of the social state. It appears in all statistics available internationally: Those states that have known the principle of the social state are those that have not only been the richest, not only the most socially balanced, but also the most productive states on earth. It should remain the same, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] This principle of the social state is particularly important when the budgetary margin is getting tighter and when it comes to defining who has to make a greater contribution so that the budget does not run out of the row. That is why, of course, the tax packages decided by the Black and Blue Federal Government are a crucial element in assessing whether Austria has become more or less social. The question that can be raised quite openly is: Does an average Austrian worker contribute more to this saving course of the Federal Government, or, for example, does Mr Prinzhorn contribute more to this saving course of the Federal Government? If you analyze what has been done here, that on the one hand, the non-social taxation of accident pensions, this bureaucratic hospital of ambulance fees, the study fees have been introduced, that family supplements have been reduced for the unemployed, namely for the children of the poorest families in Austria, Mr Gaugg, on the other hand, but no additional contribution has been requested by people like Mr Prinzhorn, then I say, ladies and gentlemen, that is a social gap which is causing social stability in economically difficult times! – It was therefore the wrong way, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] What a burden this government could have had if it had also asked for their fair contribution from people like Mr Prinzhorn and Mr Bartenstein! It could have saved itself the introduction of ambulance charges. It could have saved itself the unsocial taxation of accident pensions, and it could have saved the tuition fees. It is not to be seen that the most socially weak in our country have to pay the main burden of this burden policy, only because people like Mr. Prinzhorn do not make their fair contribution to our social system. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! As a propaganda for this whole burden policy, the Federal Government has always pointed out that it is about achieving the zero deficit. Everything else doesn't matter to her, she said. It does not care about employment, it does not care about economic growth, it does not care about social balance. It is only about the zero deficit that everything has to be subordinated to. [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] What is the result, ladies and gentlemen? – Not even what has been sold to the population as the only target, if it may be absurdly separated from other economic goals, not even this single objective, namely the zero deficit, is now achieved by this government. That means: The objective of zero deficit is not met. The zero deficit was only good for carrying out a massive burden on small and medium-sized income earners in Austria and for reducing the social state. That was really not necessary, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] There are socially sound budgetary reforms, and other Western European countries have shown this. It is possible to make a budget recovery socially compatible if you invest in jobs by investing in those companies that actually create jobs, because by increasing employment there is more tax revenue on the one hand, and on the other hand, you save certain expenditure from unemployment insurance. A socially just budget reform is aimed at job creation and not at social reduction, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. That would have been the better way for Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] After it is obvious that it cannot have been the zero deficit after this goal has also been achieved, the question arises, what is the actual background for this massive wave of stress at the expense of the more socially weak in our country, which was carried out by the black and blue government. [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] It's crystal clear what the blue-black government wants: She does not want a red and white-red way for all Austrians, she wants the introduction of a two-class society. You have started with a two-class system in medicine, where you want to get there with greater self retention, that good health care can only afford more well-being. They continue with a two-class education system where you want to make access to education more and more dependent on money and not on the commitment and the "hurncastel" of the students. They continue a policy in the pension sector where you want to split the pension system where the employment situation and solidarity in our society are no longer the basis for pensions, but they want people to deliver speculation on international stock markets. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The two-class society of the black and blue government, which it would be able to introduce after a next election, if it had the opportunity to continue with unrestricted hardness, is the true background of its stress rate. This popular desire is rightly a rejection of this course, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] We from the Socialist Group take seriously the content underlying this popular desire and the objectives. Therefore, we, which we feel committed to a society, wishing to offer fair opportunities for all in our society, will also take up the content of this popular desire. Therefore, social democracy will work to ensure that, on the one hand, the social impact assessment is introduced in Austria, in order to ensure that each measure is carefully monitored as to whether it creates a social gap in our country. Secondly, we will work to ensure that Austria gets away from the black and blue course of the two-class society and returns to a red and white-red course of solidarity and the social state, with which Austria has also grown up, ladies and gentlemen!

Professor Grünewald will give me a right, knowing the composition of the official doctors of the Austrian district administration authorities, if I say that among them are very many general physicians and a few professionals. It is not intended to question orthopaedic orthopaedic interests and to have these questions answered, nor is it intended to allow a specialist in neck, nose and ear diseases to assess problems that are clearly in the field of internalistic or neurological diseases. I believe that, with all caution, which should also be exercised in the assessment of the medical assessment system and the problem to be dealt with by the doctors in this area – the conduct of a fair assessment of the best of knowledge and conscience, taking account of the legal guidelines and in accordance with the applicable framework conditions – it is still unmistakable that we are facing a number of complaints in the field of medical reviews today. The Volksanwälte – we know it from the television broadcasts – the corresponding ombudsmen of the daily newspapers and other places in this Republic, such as the patient's attorneys, have a lot of complaints in this area. Such complaints also come from the organisations that represent the disabled, and many people who want to obtain a disability pension often feel very unfair and often inhumane in the assessments. I have always taken this seriously, and I have always wanted us to come to a better regulation in this area together with the experts and with the doctors than we have today. At present, we have some 2,500 Austrians who are subject to a review in accordance with a new procedure, which has been prepared by a group in my home with the participation of relevant experts from all relevant areas, and we will see, after the deadline provided for here, whether we are able to make this environment more humane than it is today. I therefore believe that some things can be changed in this area, with the establishment of mobile services and of qualified experts in Austria, together with the federal states. We will all be able to make a final assessment only when it works. I also say quite clearly: We also know that, due to the situation in the settled area in Austria, we have some regions and some administrative districts in which we do not have a sufficient number of experts today, and perhaps not even tomorrow, because of the bottlenecks in some areas of specialist training. In these areas, it will be safe to consider whether, under certain circumstances, the regional hospital with its experts and consiliar doctors should be included in the framework of separate contracts so that this gap can be closed. In my view, all these things must be taken into account in the drafting process, and I believe that the Secretary of State has, as has been mentioned several times, clearly represented all these considerations in the Committee in the appropriate form. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that an important area has been addressed here. I myself, as a Federal Minister, am not satisfied with today's situation in view of the many difficulties that occur in this area – be it through the legal system, by the lack of adequate interpretation, for example, of the relevant assessment directives, whether in the context of daily practice. I therefore take all these things seriously and hope that by implementing the directives we will come together to a better system that does not make it necessary to send the disabled in Austria to any central offices, but that it will allow these assessments to be carried out as close as possible to their place of residence. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e14002	Mr President. Mr Bartenstein! Ladies and gentlemen! In advance, I would like to clarify – so that there are no misunderstandings in the later discussion – that: The SPÖ supports, in principle, easier access to commerce, because it stimulates the creation of enterprises in the medium-sized area, because it allows jobs to be created or because, instead of market destabilisations, fair competition ultimately also provides more advantages for employees, for the economy and also for consumers. I am of the opinion that unjustified competition protection should be removed in the trade regulation and that regulations should also be reduced to a necessary level. – I think that we are probably quite agreed on this point. On the other hand, this, in principle, positive access to a further liberalisation of the trade regulations does not mean that we will also, at the same time and, so to speak, automatically agree with the present draft of the trade regulation. That is why we are not doing this, ladies and gentlemen, because we believe that this draft still contains a number of criticisms. For example, it is obvious that no one has made a real effort to systematically search through the trade regulations for unfair competition protection. That means, in the end, it is once again such that individual interest groups prevail, while others remain on the track. The dentists, for example, have once again asserted themselves to the dental technicians. The smokers obviously praise better than the inquisitors; the nutritionists or the mechatronics seem to have to take a step back on liberalisation at all. – To mention just a few examples. That means: Whoever is better at lobbying is obviously advocating himself. And that's something we don't want to support from the SPÖ. Our main criticism, however, goes in a different direction, ladies and gentlemen: The particular challenge of further liberalisation of the trade system lies in the fact that the trade regulation is linked to a number of other legal instruments. This connection is particularly acute in the example of apprenticeship training: In fact, if access to a business is completely released, the qualification requirements for the training of apprentices will also be eliminated from the employer. As the only criterion, the training examination, which must be carried out in accordance with the vocational training law, remains practically a purely formal act. Ladies and gentlemen! We are not ready – at a time when the quality of training is the key to professional success, at a time when good training is just a condition for having an opportunity at all on the labour market – to accept a measure that systematically undermines the quality of vocational training that is always required! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] For exactly the same reason, we are not willing to agree to a measure that will in future make it possible to train apprentices in a part-sector. We will not agree if, due to the catastrophic situation in the labour market, young people are virtually forced to take a training course in which they learn how to build up car radios or test fire extinguishers. We will not agree if young people are thus opened a sham training rail which ultimately has to go into a professional dead end. Ladies and gentlemen! It is not a question of maintaining access restrictions on trade practice, but of introducing accompanying measures in order to avoid adverse effects of in itself meaningful liberalisation measures. That is to say, it is a matter of ensuring that the qualifications for the training of apprentices are linked to professional requirements in order to avoid a reduction in the quality of training. It is therefore a matter of offering apprentices a new opportunity not only to traders and entrepreneurs, but also to young people – by guaranteeing them a qualified, high-quality training that will enable them to meet the high demands of the labour market. It is precisely that, Minister Bartenstein, which we would have expected from a minister responsible for the first time for business and work. I think you can be expected to take the same commitment, with which you take steps towards liberalisation on the one hand, on the other hand also for the opportunities of young people and apprentices. – That is exactly what you, Federal Minister Bartenstein, have not fulfilled with this innovation on the trade order. This has been distributed from choice to choice one sugar after another. In addition, within the system of an ever wider shift, it was possible to achieve unannounceability, which was originally intended only for a few top officials who were directly subordinated to a sovereign. This is not to be kept for a long time. If the average pension period of a man in 1970 is less than 5 years, this has now increased to 16 years; in women it is similar to that of a lot higher. If today a more than sixty-six-year-old comes to three Austrians of working age, it will be two in 30 years. In this situation one had to throw the row around, and at this point I would like to draw my hat in front of Mrs. Federal Minister Hostasch, who first dared to ask for an opinion on this issue – of course by a foreigner, for it is said: nemo propheta in patria – an opinion that should open the eyes of wide circles, but apparently does not have it, because the action of the Austrian Pensionist Association is still carried by the salamitactics and the foundry principle. On the other hand, the Austrian Seniors' Federation has recently decided on a position paper which takes account of this basic paradigm change, which is necessary. The main objective of rehabilitation of our overall system must be to reduce the disproportionate relationship between the number of assets and the number of pensioners. That means that the average – I emphasize: the average! – pension age needs to be increased. It follows – and I refer to today’s urgent request – that everything needs to be done that healthy active people cannot be sent into retirement. However, this is complicated by a social democracy personnel policy, which in the three areas mentioned has led to far-reaching levels of personnel, the so-called well-appointed rights of such staff, and the forced early retirement of public funding. This has certainly been the case in the private sector and there, but it is in no way comparable to what we are discussing today. The most important objective must therefore be to introduce a new way of thinking, not the one that is generally raised up for all the retirement age, but the one that we specifically pursue a pension policy, which is particularly beneficial to all those who are in particular in need, because of the fact that the average age increases for all healthy people. These needy ones include, honestly, sick people, heavy workers, who in future would have to be granted early retirement without any corresponding deductions, and undoubtedly also all those women and mothers who have taken years of child rearing or years of care of the disabled or of the elderly. High house! The better it succeeds to raise the de facto pension age for healthy people, the sooner we will be able to meet those who really need it in the coming years. High house! We should keep this in mind, and if that is the case, it will be easier to find appropriate solutions to the problems facing us today. The Austrian Senior Confederation is willing to participate in this necessary paradigm change.

With the amendment tabled today, we have achieved a further legal certainty, namely the regulation, which states: What is a processed product, and what is an original product? – A processed product is of course also treated differently in tax law and social security law. [[Calls to the Greens.]] So far, we have only been able to rely on a decree from the Minister of Finance, but it has not, so to speak, provided legal certainty for the trade regulations. . Now, with this regulation empowerment of the Minister for Economic Affairs, we have this legal certainty for the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Another area is very important to me because there has been a very strong criminalisation in the past. Until now, it was not possible to place lemonades or beer in bottles on pastures during the alm management to visitors. One was allowed to offer a cheese jause, a bacon or even, if one produced the sausage itself, a sausage, but only milk or homemade fruit juices were to be offered. It has also finally been achieved that the farmer can offer the self-burned, own snaps directly, in small quantities to the guest. [[Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] So far, it was only possible to sell a bottle as a whole and add the glasses to it. [[General hustle and bustle.]] [[Abg. Marizzi: That's where the chickens laugh!]] – These are, of course, regulations that were urgently needed. Overall, I can say that this business regulation brings about a liberalisation of professional access, an extension of the rights of trade operators, a reduction of bureaucratic barriers, a creation of incentives for self-employed jobs and a total strengthening of Austria's economic location. I am therefore glad that we can now decide on this innovation in the trade order.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e22852	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Federal Minister! I should like to say that with the many areas covered by this reform of the trade regulation, it has been possible to make a significant step towards greater market proximity and liberalisation in the field of professions and SMEs. In my speech, however, I would like to limit myself to the rules on financial service providers which are now to be decided. Ladies and gentlemen! In Article I, § 94 Z. 75 of the Industrial Regulation was created a new provision for asset advice. As you know, there are two types of asset advice: one that is subject to commercial concession, and another, rather general form. To a large extent, financial services are used with the assistance of financial service assistants subject to the concession obligation. This means that a concessiond company also has the opportunity to use freelance services from related sectors. So far, the regulation has stated that, although most of these people have actually pursued a different business activity under commercial law, they have been used as a financial service provider with a concession obligation. This has led to unwarranted hardships because many of these financial service providers have been subject to a contract agreement arrangement. With this reform approach, this abuse has been eliminated. There is now only one social security right for most financial service providers, namely the commercial one. This is good and in order, and I think it is an essential step forward for an industry that is greatly expanding for the benefit of the Austrian investment market. Second point: I believe it is necessary – and this has also been taken into account – for insurance agents and insurance brokers to clarify the terms and clarify what insurance agents and insurance brokers should and should not do. In the past, the demarcation was relatively difficult. I also think it is good that the term "insurance agent" is defined more clearly than in the past. – This has also been taken into account. To sum up, I do not consider the reform of the trade regulation to be a milestone, but a further step in the right direction to successfully place financial products and the rules of orderly supervision of the financial market in accordance with the market. I should also like to thank the Ministry of Economic Affairs for its support, but I should also like to say that the Ministry of Finance has kindly agreed to this scheme and would like to ask you to give your consent to this amendment.

So it is exactly the opposite of what was claimed before. One should not only read Mr Cap's press services, but also research more intensively what is really inside. A few words on the Reorganisation Support Act itself, whose main focus is in other areas. Among other things, it creates the conditions for the restructuring of the federal army, which, especially in the "military" area of the ministry, with the function of the head of the General Staff and the re-integration of one of today's and today's requirements, creates an adequate structure of the Federal Force. The necessary changes will be implemented, and an overdue adaptation to the situation will be made, which for the first time – or for a long time again – represents a real military reform and not just "revelations" as in the past great coalition with the military division new, new, completely new and so on. Here not only forces are reduced, but the structure of the federal army is adapted to the future tasks after the new doctrine. It is precisely what Mr Gaál said: The management structure becomes slimmer, tighter and thus more effective. This is one of the many preparations that have been made in the last two years to make this reform possible. These include legal requirements. For example, in the Military Power Act, the powers of the Federal Government have been re-regulated for the first time in important areas. To date, for example, there has not even been a clear legal regime for the capture of aircraft. For the first time, it was really explicitly created here. Furthermore, the acquisition of helicopters and transport aircraft, the "Ulan" flows to the force, new pioneer equipment and apparatus for the protection of disasters was acquired, and soon the interception question will finally be solved after a more than decades of circumference. Mr Scheibner, I congratulate you on this. Make Please keep it that way!

The debate on the previous year's VfGH knowledge has shown how strongly emotionalised this issue is being dealt with, and this fact alone shows that a generous and benevolent attitude towards minorities cannot be mentioned for a long time, although in recent years some legal steps have been taken to improve the protection of minorities. I would remind you of the State objective, the framework convention, the Charter of Regional and Minority Languages. However, I have believed that overall a more socially favourable climate would be given to an emotionless, objective debate on the rights and position of minorities, but, as has been said, the December VfGH knowledge has taught us all a better one that is not. This has not only shown the threats and evasions towards the President of the Constitutional Court – I recall the unworthy debate about President Adamovich and the racist wordplay with his name, for example, the evasion of "the Hermelin" for example – but also the threats and aggressions against the minority itself have shown this. I recall the change in the name of the signposts, the threat of shortening kindergarten support or severing support, or that it would be enslaved to Article 7 of the State Treaty. – So much to this aspect. This Green request therefore has its right, and the minority also sees it as such, namely that such a secret minority determination would in fact be an act of violence and is perceived as a threat. However, there is also a legal dimension. With the in-force step of the 1980 Volksthältungsgesetz – that is what the colleague Stoisits has already said – the first major piece of the Volksthältungsgesetz was repealed in 1950, but the second and third main piece in its version remained in force, but § 10 of these rules is linked to the fact that the Federal Government, in accordance with § 9 of the Volksthältsgestellungsgesetz, arranges such a secret minority determination by regulation. Now there is no longer this § 9 in the current national census law in 1980, there is no comparable provision, and therefore there is no legal basis for such a secret finding of the family language to be arranged at all. That is to say, these rules are actually obsolete. Of course, it does not hurt if they are now formally eliminated from the law. In summary, however, it is certain that there is currently no valid legal basis for a secret collection of the minority language. I think this is also good, especially in view of the many contradictory facts and contributions to this matter in recent months. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e28757	As the next speaker, Mr Krüger is speaking. Voluntary speaking time limit: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e28779	Mr President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The legal bases of the census are anything but ideal. In this respect, I can give my previous speakers the right. If you look at the basics, you will realize that on the one hand there is the 1950 census law and, in parallel, another law, even the same name, dating back to 1980. This is actually a legistic curiosum, that there are two equal laws that have different paragraphs that bear the same number, but say something completely different. However, in the matter itself, i.e. the secret collection of the mother tongue, is, I think, a lot to say. I do not think it is necessarily logical to conclude, as Mrs. Stoisits says, that from the findings of the Constitutional Court of December 2001 on the People's Group Law/Topography Regulation, it is imperative to conclude a kind of annulment or abolition of the secret collection of the mother tongue. I should like to remind you that the Constitutional Court has dealt with the question of the percentage from which a mixed population can be derived in a particular settlement area. I should like to remind you that the relevant Article 7 of the Vienna State Treaty provides for areas with Slovenian populations and those with mixed populations and that the Constitutional Court considered that the 25 percent tax burden was too high. But if the Constitutional Court now assumes that there is indeed a lower obstacle, then you have to ask yourself how to objectively do so. Therefore, I am not a priority of the opinion that a secret collection of the mother tongue is something negative, because the secret collection of the mother tongue was originally from its basic point of view – I bled back to 1976, as the sole government of Kreisky III – there was a kind of protection standard for the minority, so that in the course of a secret finding each subjectively could confess to a minority or not. The international term "minority" refers to the confession, that is to the subjective attitude, and not to the objective attitude. I mean, an objective assessment of whether someone masters the Slovenian mother tongue or is powerful in the German language would not be desirable. This can only be subjective. I am already wondering how such a knowledge should be effective if there is no suitable way of finding out if there are mixed Slovenian areas. But that will be all the subject of the deliberations in the Constitutional Committee. From my point of view, however, it is not that this secret collection of the mother tongue would be a priori bad and negative.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-13-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00107_d7e28941	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of this proposal is to increase direct democracy. Firstly, the public prosecutors should be given the opportunity to report special reports in addition to their annual reports. She has already done it this year, and there has been – harmlessly formulated – a little disagreement about it. I believe that it is an important and necessary measure and task that, if the public prosecutor is already so diligent and demonstrates abuses occurring in their area, the public's concerns remain in a report and inform us as parliamentarians. This is something we should take up, because we do not have this direct contact with the population: The People's Procuratorate sees the impact of various laws on the population immediately. I therefore believe that it would be important and necessary for the public prosecutor's office to be given the opportunity for the special reports to be dealt with accordingly. I would also like to respond to Mrs. Volksanwälter Bauer's wish for these special reports to be dealt with by the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives. - I think that's a good idea. This committee is an interface between the citizens and Parliament. I welcome the concerns expressed by the lawyers – especially the concern expressed by the lawyer Rosemarie Bauer – and therefore this request to amend Article 148g. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] The second point is the proposal that citizens' initiatives should not take place until the 19th century. ==References====External links== It is possible to bring in years of life. I mean, that's important. It would be a signal to young people in our country to participate actively in politics and would make political education possible in the best sense of the word for young people. I therefore think it would be good and important that this instrument of citizens' initiatives should be used by people from the 16th century onwards. It can be used as a year of life. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] The third point would be important in the light of the appreciation of the concerns expressed by citizens in the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives. Ladies and gentlemen! If this request is dealt with in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, I really hope you will agree to this very important concern if your demands, which you repeatedly make correct, are more than just lip recognition, and if you are concerned with us by the Committee on Citizens' Initiatives and Petitions. Thank you.
